WA uses cost-plus pricing and has an average selling price of $200 per item\r\n    of furniture. This price is higher than many of its competitors. Each year WA introduces new designs\r\n    of furniture. In 2018, WA’s profit fell and the directors are worried about this. The directors want to\r\n    increase productivity as a way to improve profit. The Operations Director is considering purchasing\r\n    computer-controlled machines.\r\n\r\n    Individual furniture designs, when requested by a customer, are made using job production. WA uses cost-plus pricing and has an average selling price of $200 per item\r\n    of furniture. This price is higher than many of its competitors. Each year WA introduces new designs\r\n    of furniture. In 2018, WA’s profit fell and the directors are worried about this. The directors want to\r\n    increase productivity as a way to improve profit. The Operations Director is considering purchasing\r\n    computer-controlled machines.\r\n\r\n    Individual furniture designs, when requested by a customer, are made using job production. These\r\n    items of furniture have an average selling price of $250. This will allow BB to sell its handbags all over the country.\r\nWe will need to spend a lot of money on advertising in magazines aimed at people on high incomes.\r\nThe economy has been growing rapidly and many people now have high incomes.\r\n\r\nOption B:\r\nTo accept a contract to manufacture luxury handbags for a major global business. This global business\r\nwill then sell these handbags using its own channels of distribution across the world. The global\r\nbusiness has a recognised luxury brand and its handbags sell at very high prices in exclusive shops\r\nall over the world. BB employs workers from the local community to\r\n  manufacture the handbags and belts.\r\n\r\n  BB uses batch production. The production process has waste\r\n  materials and these are put into the nearby river. However, local people complain that it affects their\r\n  drinking water.\r\n\r\n  The directors want to increase profit. They are thinking of either increasing prices or making some\r\n  of the handbags out of a material that looks like leather but is much cheaper to buy. The Marketing\r\n  Director thinks the demand for their handbags is price inelastic as many consumers are loyal to the\r\n  BB brand.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                                Appendix 1\r\n\r\n                                      Organisation chart for BB\r\n\r\n                                     Abdullah (Managing Director)\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n  Marketing Director          Operations Director        Human Resources Director       Finance Director\r\n\r\n\r\n Sales           Purchasing    Factory manager              Administration officer    Financial accountant\r\nmanager           manager                                            [1]\r\n\r\nSales staff      Assistant         Production                                              Assistant\r\n    [3]             [1]             workers                                                   [1]\r\n                                     [100]\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n  © UCLES 2017                              0450/22/INSERT/F/M/17\r\n\f                                                      3\r\n\r\n                                                Appendix 2\r\n\r\nFrom:    Abdullah\r\nTo:      BB directors\r\n\r\nI want BB to expand and I am considering the following two options.\r\n\r\nOption A:\r\nSell BB branded handbags to wholesalers. This will allow BB to sell its handbags all over the country.\r\nWe will need to spend a lot of money on advertising in magazines aimed at people on high incomes.\r\nThe economy has been growing rapidly and many people now have high incomes.\r\n\r\nOption B:\r\nTo accept a contract to manufacture luxury handbags for a major global business. This global business\r\nwill then sell these handbags using its own channels of distribution across the world. The global\r\nbusiness has a recognised luxury brand and its handbags sell at very high prices in exclusive shops\r\nall over the world. All the workers will need to be skilled and fully trained as the highest quality will\r\nbe expected. New equipment costing $20 000 will need to be purchased. BB will still make some BB\r\nbranded handbags.\r\n\r\nWe must meet to discuss which option you think we should choose.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                                Appendix 3\r\n\r\n                                    Summary of BB balance sheet ($000)\r\n\r\n                                                             2015                2016\r\n\r\n         Non-current assets                                  100                  120\r\n\r\n         Current assets:\r\n         Inventories                                           10                  40\r\n         Cash                                                  10                  10\r\n         Trade receivables                                     10                  40\r\n\r\n         Current liabilities:\r\n         Trade payables                                        40                  50\r\n\r\n         Non-current liabilities:\r\n         Long-term bank loan                                   80                  80\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2017                                 0450/22/INSERT/F/M/17\r\n\f                                                                               4\r\n\r\n                                                                    BLANK PAGE\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\nPermission to reproduce items where third-party owned material protected by copyright is included has been sought and cleared where possible. She knows that technology is important to keep the business\r\nefficient and competitive.\r\n\r\nSC employs 300 workers on the production line. There are two shifts each day: an early shift of 8 hours\r\nand a late shift of 8 hours. There is a 6-day working week. Few holidays are allowed apart from national\r\nholidays. The workers complain that the working conditions are not good.\r\n\r\nThe directors want the business to expand by starting to export cars to nearby countries. Finance is\r\navailable for this expansion.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 1\r\n\r\n                        Advert for SC produced by an advertising The hotel is located\r\n    in the centre of Main City which is 50 km from Downtown. It is near to restaurants, a train station\r\n    and the airport. It is for sale at a price of $200 000. It has not been redecorated for many\r\n    years and needs updating if it is to attract tourists from other countries. The estimated cost of\r\n    updating the hotel is $50 000. The hotel is for sale at a price of $100 000 as it is in a good location\r\n    on the beach.\r\n\r\n    XYZ Hotel\r\n\r\n    This is a modern hotel and has recently been redecorated to a high standard. The hotel is located\r\n    in the centre of Main City which is 50 km from Downtown. It is near to restaurants, a train station\r\n    and the airport. It is for sale at a price of $200 000. Technology is widely used in the hotel such as\r\n    a computer based booking system and electronic key cards to access rooms and elevators.\r\n\r\n They carry out all the management functions in the business\r\n    and there are two office workers to support them. As WA is a small business it buys in services\r\n    from other businesses such as transport companies.\r\n\r\n    WA makes many different designs of wooden furniture. WA only buys wood from forests that use\r\n    sustainable methods so that new trees are planted when others are cut down. The cost of this wood\r\n    is high but the directors think that this cost is justified as WA aims to be environmentally friendly.\r\n\r\n    WA employs 20 highly skilled production workers in the factory. Each worker specialises in one\r\n    task. All employees in the factory and the office are paid an hourly wage that is 10% above the\r\n    legal minimum wage.\r\n\r\n    WA uses batch production for most furniture designs with machinery that was purchased when the\r\n    business was started. WA uses cost-plus pricing and has an average selling price of $200 per item\r\n    of furniture. This price is higher than many of its competitors. Each year WA introduces new designs\r\n    of furniture. In 2018, WA’s profit fell and the directors are worried about this. The directors want to\r\n    increase productivity as a way to improve profit. The Operations Director is considering purchasing\r\n    computer-controlled machines.\r\n\r\n    Individual furniture designs, when requested by a customer, are made using job production. These\r\n    items of furniture have an average selling price of $250. Each item takes twice as long to make as\r\n    the furniture made by batch production.\r\n\r\n                                                 Appendix 1\r\n\r\n ES is a public limited company\r\n    which was founded 40 years ago. It has grown by taking over other smaller scooter manufacturers.\r\n    This meant that ES could use the factories and skilled employees of these other businesses. ES\r\n    is now planning to take over another scooter manufacturing business based in country Z, either\r\n    company ABC or company FGH. Financial information about these companies is shown in Appendix\r\n    1.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n    Currently ES uses a flow production method with many workers and only a few machines. The\r\n    work is boring and employees have to work long hours doing the same job. However, wages are\r\n    calculated by using time rate and this is higher than the legal minimum wage. There are no other\r\n    financial rewards for the workers.\r\n\r\n    ES currently has 30% market share in country Z. It is planning to export its products and has drawn\r\n    up a business plan for this expansion. This plan includes the promotion methods ES is considering\r\n    for its scooters in export markets. To start exporting, ES will have to increase production. It needs\r\n    to recruit 100 additional production workers and invest $10m in new technology production\r\n    machinery. This should ensure higher output as well as higher productivity.\r\n\r\n                                                Appendix 1\r\n\r\n           Financial information (2019) for the two companies ES is planning to take over.\r\n\r\n                                       ABC Company                         FGH Company\r\n\r\n     Revenue                           $100m                               $400m\r\n\r\n     Cost of sales                     $50m                                $160m\r\n\r\n     Expenses                          $40m                                $210m\r\n\r\n     Return on Capital Employed\r\n                                       20%                                 25%\r\n     (ROCE)\r\n\r\n     Current ratio                     2                                   3\r\n\r\n     Acid test ratio                   1                                   1.5\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2020                               06_0450_22_2020_1.9\r\n\f                                                 3\r\n\r\n\r\n                                           Appendix 2\r\n\r\n                               Article in the national newspaper\r\n\r\n    Main News 10 April 2020\r\n\r\n    The economy of country Z has grown rapidly over the last 5 years and incomes are high. This has\r\n    resulted in inflation. The Government is now taking action to stop inflation rising any further.\r\n    Unemployment has now started to increase. This has been welcomed by some businesses but not\r\n    all. The demand for luxury products is likely to fall if unemployment continues to rise.\r\n\r\n                                           Appendix 3\r\n\r\n                                   Email to Managing Director\r\n\r\n    To: Managing Director\r\n    From: Finance Director\r\n    Date: 20 April 2020\r\n\r\n    Re: New laws\r\n\r\n    The Government has introduced many new laws that will have an impact on ES. These legal controls\r\n    include those affecting consumer protection, location decisions and environmental protection. We\r\n    will have to consider how each of these new laws will affect our costs and operations.\r\n\r\n    Please make sure you have carried out a full analysis of what these changes will mean for ES.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2020                             06_0450_22_2020_1.9\r\n\f                                                                               4\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                                                    BLANK PAGE\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\nPermission to reproduce items where third-party owned material protected by copyright is included has been sought and cleared where possible. He worked for a toy manufacturer for 10\r\n    years. Tandeep thinks he has enough knowledge of toys and customer needs to start his own\r\n    business.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep plans to sell a large range of toys which will include products for all ages of children from\r\n    babies through to teenagers. The toys will all be well known brands and aimed at higher income\r\n    groups. The most popular product is likely to be toy cars. Tandeep also plans to sell a range of\r\n    accessories for babies such as pushchairs (strollers) which he may have to import. Making sure\r\n    there is inventory to always meet demand is very important to the success of TT.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep has a loan of $40 000 from his family to invest in the business. He wants to set up as a\r\n    private limited company. He knows that he will need working capital to run his business. He may\r\n    offer credit to some customers for more expensive items such as nursery furniture. Tandeep knows\r\n    that there are several legal controls to protect consumers.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep has been looking at locations for his toy shop. He has identified two locations to choose\r\n    between as outlined in Appendix 1.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep will need to recruit several employees to work in his shop. He plans to recruit a shop\r\n    manager and 5 sales assistants. Tandeep will carry out all the administration. As part of the\r\n    recruitment process he will need to produce job descriptions, job specifications and then shortlist\r\n    applicants.\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 1\r\n\r\n                                Two possible locations for Tandeep’s shop\r\n\r\n    Location A\r\n\r\n    The shop is located in a popular shopping mall in the city centre. Parking is expensive and the rent\r\n    is high. The mall is open from 8.00 to 22.00 every day. There are several other toy shops located\r\n    in the mall as well as banks, a cinema and children’s playground.\r\n\r\n    Location B\r\n\r\n    The shop is located on a busy street in a local town. There are many other shops and a street\r\n    market in the area. Local people frequently visit the shopping area to buy food. Parking is always\r\n    available and is free. The rent is low. There are no other toy shops in the town.\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 2\r\n\r\n                            Information on channels of distribution for toy cars\r\n\r\n {"id":"0450-2022-S-P22-Q1a","syllabus":"0450","year":2022,"series":"May/Jun","paper":2,"paper_code":"0450/22","question":"1(a)","marks":8,"question_paraphrase":"Explain two different ways TT could grow its business.","case_context":"™\r\n                 Cambridge IGCSE\r\n\r\n                 BUSINESS STUDIES                                                                              0450/22\r\n                 Paper 2 Case study                                                                     May/June 2022\r\n                 INSERT                                                                              1 hour 30 minutes\r\n\r\n                 INFORMATION\r\n                 • This insert contains the case study.\r\n*3842737973-I*\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                 • You may annotate this insert and use the blank spaces for planning. Do not write your answers on the\r\n                     insert.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                                      This document has 4 pages.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                 06_0450_22_2022_1.14\r\n                 © UCLES 2022                                                                                [Turn over\r\n\f                                                    2\r\n\r\n\r\n                                             Titantic Toys (TT)\r\n\r\n    TT is a large manufacturer of toys based in country Y. It is a public limited company and has been\r\n    trading for 20 years. TT’s products are designed for children up to the age of 5.\r\n\r\n    TT has benefited from economies of scale as it has grown. The company has been able to buy\r\n    specialist new equipment for the flow production line in its factory. TT’s factory uses lean production\r\n    methods, such as Kaizen and just-in-time inventory control. All of its employees are well-motivated\r\n    and few leave each year. The company provides training for employees in all departments of the\r\n    business whenever there are any changes such as introducing new equipment or new products.\r\n\r\n    Currently, TT only sells its products in country Y but it plans to expand into country Z and become\r\n    a multinational company. TT could buy a toy manufacturer located in country Z or build its own\r\n    factory there. The government of country Z will need to give permission for TT to build a new factory.\r\n    Either option will cost $10 million. The directors are worried about liquidity as TT expands.\r\n\r\n    TT has developed a new toy for children under 5. The toy is a soft, easy to hold doll which speaks\r\n    4 different languages. The Marketing Director thinks this new toy will help TT expand into country\r\n    Z, as there are no other products like it in that country. TT is recruiting a new Marketing Manager\r\n    for country Z. Details of the shortlisted applicants are in Appendix 3.\r\n\r\n    The marketing department has already carried out research in country Z and some results are\r\n    shown in Appendix 1.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2022                               06_0450_22_2022_1.14\r\n\f                                3\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                            Appendix 1\r\n\r\n Do not write your answers on the\r\n                     insert.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                        This document has 4 pages. Any blank pages are indicated.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                 06_0450_22_2023_1.14\r\n                 © UCLES 2023                                                                                [Turn over\r\n\f                                                     2\r\n\r\n\r\n                                         Everyone Theatre (ET)\r\n\r\nZane and Zaara are both actors. They plan to start up a theatre in a low-income area of Main City. Zane\r\nand Zaara believe that helping the community is as important as being successful entrepreneurs.\r\n\r\nThe theatre will be a private sector business and Zane and Zaara have to decide whether to start up as\r\nan unincorporated business (partnership) or as a limited company. Zane is planning to prepare a cash-flow\r\nforecast for the first year. Zaara knows they will need to set business objectives for the theatre and also\r\ndecide on a leadership style to use when managing employees. ET will have 24 part-time employees\r\nas shown in the organisational chart in Appendix 1.\r\n\r\nZane and Zaara plan to put on performances in the evenings for local people and tourists. There will be\r\n100 seats in the theatre but not all the seats will be sold if the price is too high. The Marketing Manager,\r\nZaara, will need to decide which performances to put on, what price to charge and which refreshments\r\nto sell. Zaara will also need to decide between the two possible options for the first performance when\r\nthe theatre opens. Information on the two options is shown in Appendix 2.\r\n\r\nIn the daytime the theatre building will be used by Daycare, a local social enterprise. Daycare takes care\r\nof young children up to the age of 5 so that low-income family members can go out to work. It also\r\nprovides education for the children which helps their development and progress before they start school.\r\nSome of Daycare’s objectives are different to those of ET.\r\n\r\n                                               Appendix 1\r\n\r\n                            Proposed organisational chart for the theatre\r\n\r\n                 Marketing Manager (Zaara)                            Finance and Administration\r\n                                                                           Manager (Zane)\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n       Backstage e.g.        Actors        Promotion                   Cleaners       Ticket office\r\n       costumes and           (10)            (2)                         (3)             (3)\r\n          scenery\r\n            (6)\r\n\r\n\r\n Note: The numbers in brackets are the number of part-time employees who work in each department.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2023                                06_0450_22_2023_1.14\r\n\f                                                   3\r\n\r\n\r\n                                            Appendix 2\r\n\r\n       Forecast data for the two possible options for ET’s first performance at the theatre\r\n\r\nFor each performance                                       Option 1                 Option 2\r\n\r\nNumber of actors/employees                                        7                   24\r\n\r\nAverage wage cost per actor/employee                             $30                  $10\r\n\r\nAll other costs including scenery, costumes and\r\n                                                                $1000                $2000\r\npromotion\r\n\r\nForecast revenue assuming all 100 seats are sold                $1000                $2500\r\n\r\n\r\n                                            Appendix 3\r\n\r\n                                    Email from Zane to Zaara\r\n\r\nTo: Zaara\r\n\r\nFrom: Zane\r\n\r\nDate: 1 April 2023\r\n\r\nWe want to support the local community and we need to make decisions about the following.\r\n\r\nShould we:\r\n\r\n•   Pay fair wages even though they may be higher than those paid by competing theatres?\r\n•   Only buy costumes produced She plans to start up her own\r\n       clothes shop called YC in Main City in country X. Ashanti will be a sole trader and thinks she has the\r\n       right characteristics to be a successful entrepreneur. Her target market is customers between 16 and\r\n       30 years of age.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti has written a business plan to help her when she starts YC. She has $20 000 in savings\r\n       which she could invest in the business. Ashanti needs to decide whether to use owners’ savings, a\r\n       bank loan or leasing to purchase the equipment for her shop.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti will employ an assistant manager and 3 more people to help her in the shop. She knows\r\n       that legal controls over employment may benefit her employees. Ashanti wants to make sure her\r\n       employees will be motivated, and she needs to decide the best way to do this.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti needs to decide on the product range to sell in her shop. Appendix 3 contains financial\r\n       information about the two product ranges she is considering selling. Ashanti will only sell one of these\r\n       product ranges when she first opens her shop and must decide which one to choose.\r\n\r\n\r\n       Appendix 1\r\n\r\n       Email from Ashanti’s friend to Ashanti\r\n\r\n       To:                       Ashanti\r\n       From:                     Ashanti’s friend\r\n       Date:                     2 April 2026\r\n\r\n       The marketing mix you choose for YC will be important to the success of your clothes shop. Most\r\n       people in country X live in Main City and 40% are in the 16 – 30 age group.\r\n\r\n       Here are my suggestions for the two elements of the marketing mix that you have asked me\r\n       to consider:\r\n\r\n       •            price – use competitive pricing\r\n       •            promotion – use social media.\r\n\r\n       Let me know if you think these would be suitable as part of the marketing mix for the new shop.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n       © Cambridge University Press & Assessment 2026  The latest government report forecasts economic growth of 4% in country X, as the economy moves\r\n       into the boom stage of the business cycle.\r\n\r\n       Interest rates are expected to increase from 2% to 5% in the next few months as the government tries\r\n       to stop the economy growing too quickly.\r\n\r\n\r\n       Appendix 3\r\n\r\n       Forecast information about the two She plans to start up her own\r\n       clothes shop called YC in Main City in country X. Ashanti will be a sole trader and thinks she has the\r\n       right characteristics to be a successful entrepreneur. Her target market is customers between 16 and\r\n       30 years of age.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti has written a business plan to help her when she starts YC. She has $20 000 in savings\r\n       which she could invest in the business. Ashanti needs to decide whether to use owners’ savings, a\r\n       bank loan or leasing to purchase the equipment for her shop.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti will employ an assistant manager and 3 more people to help her in the shop. She knows\r\n       that legal controls over employment may benefit her employees. Ashanti wants to make sure her\r\n       employees will be motivated, and she needs to decide the best way to do this.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti needs to decide on the product range to sell in her shop. BB currently\r\n  does not use the Internet in its business operations.\r\n\r\n  The leather used by BB has always been purchased from the same local business since BB was set\r\n  up. This supplier guarantees high quality leather and has always delivered on time.\r\n\r\n  BB was started 35 years ago and is now managed by Abdullah, the grandson of the original owner.\r\n  The other directors are all members of the family. BB employs workers from the local community to\r\n  manufacture the handbags and belts.\r\n\r\n  BB uses batch production. The workers who make the handbags and belts are skilled, but wage costs\r\n  are kept low by employing young workers to pack the products. The production process has waste\r\n  materials and these are put into the nearby river. However, local people complain that it affects their\r\n  drinking water.\r\n\r\n  The directors want to increase profit. They are thinking of either increasing prices or making some\r\n  of the handbags out of a material that looks like leather but is much cheaper to buy. The Marketing\r\n  Director thinks the demand for their handbags is price inelastic as many consumers are loyal to the\r\n  BB brand.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                                Appendix 1\r\n\r\n                                      Organisation chart for BB\r\n\r\n                                     Abdullah (Managing Director)\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n  Marketing Director          Operations Director        Human Resources Director       Finance Director\r\n\r\n\r\n Sales           Purchasing    Factory manager              Administration officer    Financial accountant\r\nmanager           manager                                            [1]\r\n\r\nSales staff      Assistant         Production                                              Assistant\r\n    [3]             [1]             workers                                                   [1]\r\n                                     [100]\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n  © UCLES 2017                              0450/22/INSERT/F/M/17\r\n\f                                                      3\r\n\r\n                                                Appendix 2\r\n\r\nFrom:    Abdullah\r\nTo:      BB directors\r\n\r\nI want BB to expand and I am considering the following two options.\r\n\r\nOption A:\r\nSell BB branded handbags to wholesalers. This will allow BB to sell its handbags all over the country.\r\nWe will need to spend a lot of money on advertising in magazines aimed at people on high incomes.\r\nThe economy has been growing rapidly and many people now have high incomes.\r\n\r\nOption B:\r\nTo accept a contract to manufacture luxury handbags for a major global business. This global business\r\nwill then sell these handbags using its own channels of distribution across the world. The global\r\nbusiness has a recognised luxury brand and its handbags sell at very high prices in exclusive shops\r\nall over the world. All the workers will need to be skilled and fully trained as the highest quality will\r\nbe expected. New equipment costing $20 000 will need to be purchased. BB will still make some BB\r\nbranded handbags.\r\n\r\nWe must meet to discuss which option you think we should choose.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                                Appendix 3\r\n\r\n                                    Summary of BB balance sheet ($000)\r\n\r\n                                                             2015                2016\r\n\r\n         Non-current assets                                  100                  120\r\n\r\n         Current assets:\r\n         Inventories                                           10                  40\r\n         Cash                                                  10                  10\r\n         Trade receivables                                     10                  40\r\n\r\n         Current liabilities:\r\n         Trade payables                                        40                  50\r\n\r\n         Non-current liabilities:\r\n         Long-term bank loan                                   80                  80\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2017                                 0450/22/INSERT/F/M/17\r\n\f                                                                               4\r\n\r\n                                                                    BLANK PAGE\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\nPermission to reproduce items where third-party owned material protected by copyright is included has been sought and cleared where possible. The global\r\nbusiness has a recognised luxury brand and its handbags sell at very high prices in exclusive shops\r\nall over the world. All the workers will need to be skilled and fully trained as the highest quality will\r\nbe expected. New equipment costing $20 000 will need to be purchased. BB will still make some BB\r\nbranded handbags.\r\n\r\nWe must meet to discuss which option you think we should choose.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                                Appendix 3\r\n\r\n                                    Summary of BB balance sheet ($000)\r\n\r\n                                                             2015                2016\r\n\r\n         Non-current assets                                  100                  120\r\n\r\n         Current assets:\r\n         Inventories                                           10                  40\r\n         Cash                                                  10                  10\r\n         Trade receivables                                     10                  40\r\n\r\n         Current liabilities:\r\n         Trade payables                                        40                  50\r\n\r\n         Non-current liabilities:\r\n         Long-term bank loan                                   80                  80\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2017                                 0450/22/INSERT/F/M/17\r\n\f                                                                               4\r\n\r\n                                                                    BLANK PAGE\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\nPermission to reproduce items where third-party owned material protected by copyright is included has been sought and cleared where possible. BB currently\r\n  does not use the Internet in its business operations.\r\n\r\n  The leather used by BB has always been purchased from the same local business since BB was set\r\n  up. This supplier guarantees high quality leather and has always delivered on time.\r\n\r\n  BB was started 35 years ago and is now managed by Abdullah, the grandson of the original owner.\r\n  The other directors are all members of the family. BB employs workers from the local community to\r\n  manufacture the handbags and belts.\r\n\r\n  BB uses batch production. The workers who make the handbags and belts are skilled, but wage costs\r\n  are kept low by employing young workers to pack the products. The production process has waste\r\n  materials and these are put into the nearby river. However, local people complain that it affects their\r\n  drinking water.\r\n\r\n  The directors want to increase profit. They are thinking of either increasing prices or making some\r\n  of the handbags out of a material that looks like leather but is much cheaper to buy. The Marketing\r\n  Director thinks the demand for their handbags is price inelastic as many consumers are loyal to the\r\n  BB brand.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                                Appendix 1\r\n\r\n                                      Organisation chart for BB\r\n\r\n                                     Abdullah (Managing Director)\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n  Marketing Director          Operations Director        Human Resources Director       Finance Director\r\n\r\n\r\n Sales           Purchasing    Factory manager              Administration officer    Financial accountant\r\nmanager           manager                                            [1]\r\n\r\nSales staff      Assistant         Production                                              Assistant\r\n    [3]             [1]             workers                                                   [1]\r\n                                     [100]\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n  © UCLES 2017                              0450/22/INSERT/F/M/17\r\n\f                                                      3\r\n\r\n                                                Appendix 2\r\n\r\nFrom:    Abdullah\r\nTo:      BB directors\r\n\r\nI want BB to expand and I am considering the following two options.\r\n\r\nOption A:\r\nSell BB branded handbags to wholesalers. To do this, each car has a\r\nbar-code that indicates how it is to be produced with slight differences from the next car. For example,\r\none car may have black paint and 2 doors and the next car red paint and 4 doors.\r\n\r\nThe quality method used is quality control. The Operations manager believes that without a good\r\nreputation the business would fail. She knows that technology is important to keep the business\r\nefficient and competitive.\r\n\r\nSC employs 300 workers on the production line. There are two shifts each day: an early shift of 8 hours\r\nand a late shift of 8 hours. There is a 6-day working week. Few holidays are allowed apart from national\r\nholidays. The workers complain that the working conditions are not good.\r\n\r\nThe directors want the business to expand by starting to export cars to nearby countries. Finance is\r\navailable for this expansion.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 1\r\n\r\n                        Advert for SC produced by an advertising It has been very successful with profit increasing each year. The demand\r\n    for hotel accommodation in country X is also growing.\r\n\r\n    DH is located next to the beach in Downtown. Half of the hotel rooms are booked for 6 months of\r\n    the year and all the rooms are booked for 4 months of the year. DH is empty for the remaining 2\r\n    months as the weather is very bad. Most of the guests that stay at the hotel are tourists from country\r\n    X on coach (bus) tours. Hilda thinks she needs to attract hotel guests all year round.\r\n\r\n    A lot of litter is left on the beach next to the hotel and Hilda does not think she should pay to keep\r\n    it clean. Local people also use the beach and it can be very busy, leading to hotel guests complaining\r\n    about overcrowding and the amount of litter such as empty drinks bottles.\r\n\r\n    Hilda wants to expand the company by taking over another hotel. There are two hotels nearby that\r\n    are for sale: ABC Hotel and XYZ Hotel.\r\n\r\n    ABC Hotel\r\n\r\n    This has been owned by a local family for over 30 years. It has not been redecorated for many\r\n    years and needs updating if it is to attract tourists from other countries. The estimated cost of\r\n    updating the hotel is $50 000. The hotel is for sale at a price of $100 000 as it is in a good location\r\n    on the beach.\r\n\r\n    XYZ Hotel\r\n\r\n    This is a modern hotel and has recently been redecorated to a high standard. The hotel is located\r\n    in the centre of Main City which is 50 km from Downtown. It is near to restaurants, a train station\r\n    and the airport. It is for sale at a price of $200 000. Technology is widely used in the hotel such as\r\n    a computer based booking system and electronic key cards to access rooms and elevators.\r\n\r\n Most of the guests that stay at the hotel are tourists from country\r\n    X on coach (bus) tours. Hilda thinks she needs to attract hotel guests all year round.\r\n\r\n    A lot of litter is left on the beach next to the hotel and Hilda does not think she should pay to keep\r\n    it clean. Local people also use the beach and it can be very busy, leading to hotel guests complaining\r\n    about overcrowding and the amount of litter such as empty drinks bottles.\r\n\r\n    Hilda wants to expand the company by taking over another hotel. There are two hotels nearby that\r\n    are for sale: ABC Hotel and XYZ Hotel.\r\n\r\n    ABC Hotel\r\n\r\n    This has been owned by a local family for over 30 years. It has not been redecorated for many\r\n    years and needs updating if it is to attract tourists from other countries. The estimated cost of\r\n    updating the hotel is $50 000. The hotel is for sale at a price of $100 000 as it is in a good location\r\n    on the beach.\r\n\r\n    XYZ Hotel\r\n\r\n    This is a modern hotel and has recently been redecorated to a high standard. The hotel is located\r\n    in the centre of Main City which is 50 km from Downtown. It is near to restaurants, a train station\r\n    and the airport. It is for sale at a price of $200 000. Technology is widely used in the hotel such as\r\n    a computer based booking system and electronic key cards to access rooms and elevators.\r\n\r\n It was started 10\r\n    years ago by the Singh family. Two brothers and one sister are the only shareholders and they are\r\n    also the directors of the company. They carry out all the management functions in the business\r\n    and there are two office workers to support them. As WA is a small business it buys in services\r\n    from other businesses such as transport companies.\r\n\r\n    WA makes many different designs of wooden furniture. WA only buys wood from forests that use\r\n    sustainable methods so that new trees are planted when others are cut down. The cost of this wood\r\n    is high but the directors think that this cost is justified as WA aims to be environmentally friendly.\r\n\r\n    WA employs 20 highly skilled production workers in the factory. Each worker specialises in one\r\n    task. All employees in the factory and the office are paid an hourly wage that is 10% above the\r\n    legal minimum wage.\r\n\r\n    WA uses batch production for most furniture designs with machinery that was purchased when the\r\n    business was started. WA uses cost-plus pricing and has an average selling price of $200 per item\r\n    of furniture. This price is higher than many of its competitors. Each year WA introduces new designs\r\n    of furniture. In 2018, WA’s profit fell and the directors are worried about this. The directors want to\r\n    increase productivity as a way to improve profit. The Operations Director is considering purchasing\r\n    computer-controlled machines.\r\n\r\n    Individual furniture designs, when requested by a customer, are made using job production. These\r\n    items of furniture have an average selling price of $250. Each item takes twice as long to make as\r\n    the furniture made by batch production.\r\n\r\n                                                 Appendix 1\r\n\r\n ES\r\n    is now planning to take over another scooter manufacturing business based in country Z, either\r\n    company ABC or company FGH. Financial information about these companies is shown in Appendix\r\n    1.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n    Currently ES uses a flow production method with many workers and only a few machines. The\r\n    work is boring and employees have to work long hours doing the same job. However, wages are\r\n    calculated by using time rate and this is higher than the legal minimum wage. There are no other\r\n    financial rewards for the workers.\r\n\r\n    ES currently has 30% market share in country Z. It is planning to export its products and has drawn\r\n    up a business plan for this expansion. This plan includes the promotion methods ES is considering\r\n    for its scooters in export markets. To start exporting, ES will have to increase production. It needs\r\n    to recruit 100 additional production workers and invest $10m in new technology production\r\n    machinery. This has\r\n    resulted in inflation. The Government is now taking action to stop inflation rising any further.\r\n    Unemployment has now started to increase. This has been welcomed by some businesses but not\r\n    all. Do not write your answers on the\r\n                     insert.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                          This document has 4 pages. Blank pages are indicated.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                 06_0450_22_2020_1.9\r\n                 © UCLES 2020                                                                                [Turn over\r\n\f                                                    2\r\n\r\n\r\n                                            Eco Scooters (ES)\r\n\r\n    ES makes scooters (small motorcycles) which are sold in country Z. ES is a public limited company\r\n    which was founded 40 years ago. It has grown by taking over other smaller scooter manufacturers.\r\n    This meant that ES could use the factories and skilled employees of these other businesses. ES\r\n    is now planning to take over another scooter manufacturing business based in country Z, either\r\n    company ABC or company FGH. Financial information about these companies is shown in Appendix\r\n    1.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n    Currently ES uses a flow production method with many workers and only a few machines. The\r\n    work is boring and employees have to work long hours doing the same job. However, wages are\r\n    calculated by using time rate and this is higher than the legal minimum wage. There are no other\r\n    financial rewards for the workers.\r\n\r\n    ES currently has 30% market share in country Z. It is planning to export its products and has drawn\r\n    up a business plan for this expansion. This plan includes the promotion methods ES is considering\r\n    for its scooters in export markets. To start exporting, ES will have to increase production. It needs\r\n    to recruit 100 additional production workers and invest $10m in new technology production\r\n    machinery. This should ensure higher output as well as higher productivity.\r\n\r\n                                                Appendix 1\r\n\r\n           Financial information (2019) for the two companies ES is planning to take over.\r\n\r\n                                       ABC Company                         FGH Company\r\n\r\n     Revenue                           $100m                               $400m\r\n\r\n     Cost of sales                     $50m                                $160m\r\n\r\n     Expenses                          $40m                                $210m\r\n\r\n     Return on Capital Employed\r\n                                       20%                                 25%\r\n     (ROCE)\r\n\r\n     Current ratio                     2                                   3\r\n\r\n     Acid test ratio                   1                                   1.5\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2020                               06_0450_22_2020_1.9\r\n\f                                                 3\r\n\r\n\r\n                                           Appendix 2\r\n\r\n                               Article in the national newspaper\r\n\r\n    Main News 10 April 2020\r\n\r\n    The economy of country Z has grown rapidly over the last 5 years and incomes are high. This has\r\n    resulted in inflation. The Government is now taking action to stop inflation rising any further.\r\n    Unemployment has now started to increase. This has been welcomed by some businesses but not\r\n    all. The demand for luxury products is likely to fall if unemployment continues to rise.\r\n\r\n                                           Appendix 3\r\n\r\n                                   Email to Managing Director\r\n\r\n    To: Managing Director\r\n    From: Finance Director\r\n    Date: 20 April 2020\r\n\r\n    Re: New laws\r\n\r\n    The Government has introduced many new laws that will have an impact on ES. These legal controls\r\n    include those affecting consumer protection, location decisions and environmental protection. We\r\n    will have to consider how each of these new laws will affect our costs and operations.\r\n\r\n    Please make sure you have carried out a full analysis of what these changes will mean for ES.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2020                             06_0450_22_2020_1.9\r\n\f                                                                               4\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                                                    BLANK PAGE\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\nPermission to reproduce items where third-party owned material protected by copyright is included has been sought and cleared where possible. Any blank pages are indicated.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                 06_0450_22_2021_1.11\r\n                 © UCLES 2021                                                                                [Turn over\r\n\f                                                     2\r\n\r\n\r\n                                            Tandeep Toys (TT)\r\n\r\n    Tandeep is planning to set up a toy shop in country Z. He worked for a toy manufacturer for 10\r\n    years. Tandeep thinks he has enough knowledge of toys and customer needs to start his own\r\n    business.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep plans to sell a large range of toys which will include products for all ages of children from\r\n    babies through to teenagers. The toys will all be well known brands and aimed at higher income\r\n    groups. The most popular product is likely to be toy cars. Tandeep also plans to sell a range of\r\n    accessories for babies such as pushchairs (strollers) which he may have to import. Making sure\r\n    there is inventory to always meet demand is very important to the success of TT.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep has a loan of $40 000 from his family to invest in the business. He wants to set up as a\r\n    private limited company. He knows that he will need working capital to run his business. He may\r\n    offer credit to some customers for more expensive items such as nursery furniture. Tandeep knows\r\n    that there are several legal controls to protect consumers.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep has been looking at locations for his toy shop. He has identified two locations to choose\r\n    between as outlined in Appendix 1.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep will need to recruit several employees to work in his shop. He plans to recruit a shop\r\n    manager and 5 sales assistants. Tandeep will carry out all the administration. As part of the\r\n    recruitment process he will need to produce job descriptions, job specifications and then shortlist\r\n    applicants.\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 1\r\n\r\n                                Two possible locations for Tandeep’s shop\r\n\r\n    Location A\r\n\r\n    The shop is located in a popular shopping mall in the city centre. Parking is expensive and the rent\r\n    is high. The mall is open from 8.00 to 22.00 every day. There are several other toy shops located\r\n    in the mall as well as banks, a cinema and children’s playground.\r\n\r\n    Location B\r\n\r\n    The shop is located on a busy street in a local town. There are many other shops and a street\r\n    market in the area. Local people frequently visit the shopping area to buy food. Parking is always\r\n    available and is free. The rent is low. There are no other toy shops in the town.\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 2\r\n\r\n                            Information on channels of distribution for toy cars\r\n\r\n                                               Wholesaler in country Z       Manufacturer in country Z\r\n\r\n     Unit cost of toy car                      $15                           $5\r\n\r\n     Delivery time for orders                  1 day                         4 weeks\r\n\r\n     Minimum quantity that must be ordered 10 units                          1000 units\r\n\r\n     Trade credit                              1 month                       3 months\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2021                               06_0450_22_2021_1.11\r\n\f He worked for a toy manufacturer for 10\r\n    years. Tandeep thinks he has enough knowledge of toys and customer needs to start his own\r\n    business.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep plans to sell a large range of toys which will include products for all ages of children from\r\n    babies through to teenagers. The toys will all be well known brands and aimed at higher income\r\n    groups. The most popular product is likely to be toy cars. Tandeep also plans to sell a range of\r\n    accessories for babies such as pushchairs (strollers) which he may have to import. Making sure\r\n    there is inventory to always meet demand is very important to the success of TT.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep has a loan of $40 000 from his family to invest in the business. He wants to set up as a\r\n    private limited company. He knows that he will need working capital to run his business. He may\r\n    offer credit to some customers for more expensive items such as nursery furniture. Tandeep knows\r\n    that there are several legal controls to protect consumers.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep has been looking at locations for his toy shop. He has identified two locations to choose\r\n    between as outlined in Appendix 1.\r\n\r\n    Tandeep will need to recruit several employees to work in his shop. He plans to recruit a shop\r\n    manager and 5 sales assistants. Tandeep will carry out all the administration. As part of the\r\n    recruitment process he will need to produce job descriptions, job specifications and then shortlist\r\n    applicants.\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 1\r\n\r\n                                Two possible locations for Tandeep’s shop\r\n\r\n    Location A\r\n\r\n    The shop is located in a popular shopping mall in the city centre. Parking is expensive and the rent\r\n    is high. The mall is open from 8.00 to 22.00 every day. There are several other toy shops located\r\n    in the mall as well as banks, a cinema and children’s playground.\r\n\r\n    Location B\r\n\r\n    The shop is located on a busy street in a local town. There are many other shops and a street\r\n    market in the area. Local people frequently visit the shopping area to buy food. Parking is always\r\n    available and is free. The rent is low. There are no other toy shops in the town.\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 2\r\n\r\n                            Information on channels of distribution for toy cars\r\n\r\n It is a public limited company and has been\r\n    trading for 20 years. TT’s products are designed for children up to the age of 5.\r\n\r\n    TT has benefited from economies of scale as it has grown. The company has been able to buy\r\n    specialist new equipment for the flow production line in its factory. TT’s factory uses lean production\r\n    methods, such as Kaizen and just-in-time inventory control. All of its employees are well-motivated\r\n    and few leave each year. The company provides training for employees in all departments of the\r\n    business whenever there are any changes such as introducing new equipment or new products.\r\n\r\n    Currently, TT only sells its products in country Y but it plans to expand into country Z and become\r\n    a multinational company. TT could buy a toy manufacturer located in country Z or build its own\r\n    factory there. The government of country Z will need to give permission for TT to build a new factory.\r\n    Either option will cost $10 million. The directors are worried about liquidity as TT expands.\r\n\r\n    TT has developed a new toy for children under 5. The toy is a soft, easy to hold doll which speaks\r\n    4 different languages. The Marketing Director thinks this new toy will help TT expand into country\r\n    Z, as there are no other products like it in that country. TT is recruiting a new Marketing Manager\r\n    for country Z. Do not write your answers on the\r\n                     insert.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                                      This document has 4 pages.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                 06_0450_22_2022_1.14\r\n                 © UCLES 2022                                                                                [Turn over\r\n\f                                                    2\r\n\r\n\r\n                                             Titantic Toys (TT)\r\n\r\n    TT is a large manufacturer of toys based in country Y. It is a public limited company and has been\r\n    trading for 20 years. TT’s products are designed for children up to the age of 5.\r\n\r\n    TT has benefited from economies of scale as it has grown. The company has been able to buy\r\n    specialist new equipment for the flow production line in its factory. TT’s factory uses lean production\r\n    methods, such as Kaizen and just-in-time inventory control. All of its employees are well-motivated\r\n    and few leave each year. The company provides training for employees in all departments of the\r\n    business whenever there are any changes such as introducing new equipment or new products.\r\n\r\n    Currently, TT only sells its products in country Y but it plans to expand into country Z and become\r\n    a multinational company. TT could buy a toy manufacturer located in country Z or build its own\r\n    factory there. The government of country Z will need to give permission for TT to build a new factory.\r\n    Either option will cost $10 million. The directors are worried about liquidity as TT expands.\r\n\r\n    TT has developed a new toy for children under 5. The toy is a soft, easy to hold doll which speaks\r\n    4 different languages. The Marketing Director thinks this new toy will help TT expand into country\r\n    Z, as there are no other products like it in that country. TT is recruiting a new Marketing Manager\r\n    for country Z. Details of the shortlisted applicants are in Appendix 3.\r\n\r\n    The marketing department has already carried out research in country Z and some results are\r\n    shown in Appendix 1.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2022                               06_0450_22_2022_1.14\r\n\f                                3\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                            Appendix 1\r\n\r\n Zane is planning to prepare a cash-flow\r\nforecast for the first year. Zaara knows they will need to set business objectives for the theatre and also\r\ndecide on a leadership style to use when managing employees. ET will have 24 part-time employees\r\nas shown in the organisational chart in Appendix 1.\r\n\r\nZane and Zaara plan to put on performances in the evenings for local people and tourists. There will be\r\n100 seats in the theatre but not all the seats will be sold if the price is too high. The Marketing Manager,\r\nZaara, will need to decide which performances to put on, what price to charge and which refreshments\r\nto sell. Zaara will also need to decide between the two possible options for the first performance when\r\nthe theatre opens. Information on the two options is shown in Appendix 2.\r\n\r\nIn the daytime the theatre building will be used by Daycare, a local social enterprise. Daycare takes care\r\nof young children up to the age of 5 so that low-income family members can go out to work. It also\r\nprovides education for the children which helps their development and progress before they start school.\r\nSome of Daycare’s objectives are different to those of ET.\r\n\r\n Zane is planning to prepare a cash-flow\r\nforecast for the first year. Zaara knows they will need to set business objectives for the theatre and also\r\ndecide on a leadership style to use when managing employees. ET will have 24 part-time employees\r\nas shown in the organisational chart in Appendix 1.\r\n\r\nZane and Zaara plan to put on performances in the evenings for local people and tourists. There will be\r\n100 seats in the theatre but not all the seats will be sold if the price is too high. The Marketing Manager,\r\nZaara, will need to decide which performances to put on, what price to charge and which refreshments\r\nto sell. Zaara will also need to decide between the two possible options for the first performance when\r\nthe theatre opens. Information on the two options is shown in Appendix 2.\r\n\r\nIn the daytime the theatre building will be used by Daycare, a local social enterprise. Daycare takes care\r\nof young children up to the age of 5 so that low-income family members can go out to work. It also\r\nprovides education for the children which helps their development and progress before they start school.\r\nSome of Daycare’s objectives are different to those of ET.\r\n\r\n One of JJ’s objectives is for the business to expand\r\nand another objective is to increase profit each year.\r\n\r\nJJ manufactures all its jewellery in a factory in country X. All raw materials are purchased from local\r\nsuppliers. JJ has 60 full-time and 25 part-time employees to make the jewellery. Most of these employees\r\nare skilled.\r\n\r\nJJ’s jewellery is beautiful but not expensive and therefore affordable for many people. The business has\r\nmany competitors. JJ currently sells 70% of its products in country X through jewellery shops. The other\r\n30% are sold using ecommerce and sold directly to customers in other countries. Demand for JJ’s\r\njewellery is continuing to increase.\r\n\r\nJJ’s Marketing Manager is planning to launch a new item of jewellery in country X and needs to choose\r\nbetween two products. Information on these two products is shown in Appendix 1. They will also need\r\nto decide the most effective way to promote the new product in country X.\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 1\r\n\r\n                  Information to calculate break-even for Product A and Product B\r\n\r\n                                                          Product A                 Product B\r\n\r\nType of product                                   Bracelet                   Ring\r\n\r\nFixed cost for each product per week              $50 000                    $50 000\r\n\r\nSelling price per product                         $50                        $120\r\n\r\nVariable cost per product                         $25                        $40\r\n\r\nForecast sales per week                           2500                       750\r\n\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 2\r\n\r\n                        Newspaper article from Main City News in country X\r\n\r\nAfter several years of economic growth the government is predicting a change in the economy this year.\r\n\r\nUnemployment is forecast to increase from 3% to 6% by the end of the year. Other jewellery\r\nmanufacturers also use coal. We know the price of coal is increasing and will continue to increase in\r\nthe future. However, the government in country X is encouraging businesses to invest in solar panels\r\nand is providing a 25% grant.\r\n\r\n• The gem stones used in our jewellery come from local mines. We have been told that the land around\r\nthese mines cannot be returned to agricultural use after the mine is closed. However, pressure groups\r\nhave told us that we can buy gems from suppliers that use alternative ways of mining which do not\r\ndamage the environment. Do not write your answers on the\r\n                     insert.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                        This document has 4 pages. Any blank pages are indicated.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                 06_0450_22_2024_1.10\r\n                 © UCLES 2024                                                                                [Turn over\r\n\f                                                   2\r\n\r\n\r\n                                         Jems Jewellery (JJ)\r\n\r\nJJ is a large manufacturer of jewellery such as bracelets, earrings and rings. JJ was set up 25 years\r\nago in country X and is a public limited company. One of JJ’s objectives is for the business to expand\r\nand another objective is to increase profit each year.\r\n\r\nJJ manufactures all its jewellery in a factory in country X. All raw materials are purchased from local\r\nsuppliers. JJ has 60 full-time and 25 part-time employees to make the jewellery. Most of these employees\r\nare skilled.\r\n\r\nJJ’s jewellery is beautiful but not expensive and therefore affordable for many people. The business has\r\nmany competitors. JJ currently sells 70% of its products in country X through jewellery shops. The other\r\n30% are sold using ecommerce and sold directly to customers in other countries. Demand for JJ’s\r\njewellery is continuing to increase.\r\n\r\nJJ’s Marketing Manager is planning to launch a new item of jewellery in country X and needs to choose\r\nbetween two products. Information on these two products is shown in Appendix 1. They will also need\r\nto decide the most effective way to promote the new product in country X.\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 1\r\n\r\n                  Information to calculate break-even for Product A and Product B\r\n\r\n                                                          Product A                 Product B\r\n\r\nType of product                                   Bracelet                   Ring\r\n\r\nFixed cost for each product per week              $50 000                    $50 000\r\n\r\nSelling price per product                         $50                        $120\r\n\r\nVariable cost per product                         $25                        $40\r\n\r\nForecast sales per week                           2500                       750\r\n\r\n\r\n                                             Appendix 2\r\n\r\n                        Newspaper article from Main City News in country X\r\n\r\nAfter several years of economic growth the government is predicting a change in the economy this year.\r\n\r\nUnemployment is forecast to increase from 3% to 6% by the end of the year. Increases in unemployment\r\nare expected to continue for the next two years. Many businesses will be affected by this increase.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2024                              06_0450_22_2024_1.10\r\n\f                                                  3\r\n\r\n\r\n                                            Appendix 3\r\n\r\n                      Email from Managing Director to Operations Director\r\n\r\nTo: Operations Director\r\n\r\nFrom: Managing Director\r\n\r\nDate: 1 May 2024\r\n\r\nPressure groups have identified two environmental issues when we manufacture our jewellery.\r\n\r\nThese two environmental issues are:\r\n\r\n• We use coal for fuel in our factory which we know contributes to global warming. Other jewellery\r\nmanufacturers also use coal. We know the price of coal is increasing and will continue to increase in\r\nthe future. However, the government in country X is encouraging businesses to invest in solar panels\r\nand is providing a 25% grant.\r\n\r\n• The gem stones used in our jewellery come from local mines. We have been told that the land around\r\nthese mines cannot be returned to agricultural use after the mine is closed. However, pressure groups\r\nhave told us that we can buy gems from suppliers that use alternative ways of mining which do not\r\ndamage the environment. These mines are further away from our factory.\r\n\r\nWe need to respond to these issues or our sales and profit may be affected.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n© UCLES 2024                             06_0450_22_2024_1.10\r\n\f                                                                             4\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n                                                                   BLANK PAGE\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\nPermission to reproduce items where third-party owned material protected by copyright is included has been sought and cleared where possible. She plans to start up her own\r\n       clothes shop called YC in Main City in country X. Ashanti will be a sole trader and thinks she has the\r\n       right characteristics to be a successful entrepreneur. Her target market is customers between 16 and\r\n       30 years of age.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti has written a business plan to help her when she starts YC. She has $20 000 in savings\r\n       which she could invest in the business. Ashanti needs to decide whether to use owners’ savings, a\r\n       bank loan or leasing to purchase the equipment for her shop.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti will employ an assistant manager and 3 more people to help her in the shop. She knows\r\n       that legal controls over employment may benefit her employees. Ashanti wants to make sure her\r\n       employees will be motivated, and she needs to decide the best way to do this.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti needs to decide on the product range to sell in her shop. Appendix 3 contains financial\r\n       information about the two product ranges she is considering selling. Ashanti will only sell one of these\r\n       product ranges when she first opens her shop and must decide which one to choose.\r\n\r\n\r\n       Appendix 1\r\n\r\n       Email from Ashanti’s friend to Ashanti\r\n\r\n       To:                       Ashanti\r\n       From:                     Ashanti’s friend\r\n       Date:                     2 April 2026\r\n\r\n       The marketing mix you choose for YC will be important to the success of your clothes shop. Most\r\n       people in country X live in Main City and 40% are in the 16 – 30 age group.\r\n\r\n       Here are my suggestions for the two elements of the marketing mix that you have asked me\r\n       to consider:\r\n\r\n       •            price – use competitive pricing\r\n       •            promotion – use social media.\r\n\r\n       Let me know if you think these would be suitable as part of the marketing mix for the new shop.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n       © Cambridge University Press & Assessment 2026 She plans to start up her own\r\n       clothes shop called YC in Main City in country X. Ashanti will be a sole trader and thinks she has the\r\n       right characteristics to be a successful entrepreneur. Her target market is customers between 16 and\r\n       30 years of age.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti has written a business plan to help her when she starts YC. She has $20 000 in savings\r\n       which she could invest in the business. Ashanti needs to decide whether to use owners’ savings, a\r\n       bank loan or leasing to purchase the equipment for her shop.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti will employ an assistant manager and 3 more people to help her in the shop. She knows\r\n       that legal controls over employment may benefit her employees. Ashanti wants to make sure her\r\n       employees will be motivated, and she needs to decide the best way to do this.\r\n\r\n       Ashanti needs to decide on the product range to sell in her shop. Appendix 3 contains financial\r\n       information about the two product ranges she is considering selling. Ashanti will only sell one of these\r\n       product ranges when she first opens her shop and must decide which one to choose.\r\n\r\n\r\n       Appendix 1\r\n\r\n       Email from Ashanti’s friend to Ashanti\r\n\r\n       To:                       Ashanti\r\n       From:                     Ashanti’s friend\r\n       Date:                     2 April 2026\r\n\r\n       The marketing mix you choose for YC will be important to the success of your clothes shop. Most\r\n       people in country X live in Main City and 40% are in the 16 – 30 age group.\r\n\r\n       Here are my suggestions for the two elements of the marketing mix that you have asked me\r\n       to consider:\r\n\r\n       •            price – use competitive pricing\r\n       •            promotion – use social media.\r\n\r\n       Let me know if you think these would be suitable as part of the marketing mix for the new shop.\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n\r\n       © Cambridge University Press & Assessment 2026
